They went to various places, performed penance, and chanted their respective mantras. Whether sitting, eating, sleeping, or awake, they remembered Ganesha with single-minded devotion.

After a thousand divine years, Ganesha, the ocean of mercy, appeared before them in the form they had meditated upon. To some, he appeared with eight arms and the radiance of a cloud; to others, he appeared with four arms and the color of the moon; to some, he appeared as a six-armed red deity; and to others, he appeared with a thousand eyes and arms. He appeared as a child, a youth, and an old man. He was seen with ten, twelve, or eighteen arms, radiant as ten million suns, riding a mouse, a lion, or a peacock, with one or many faces.

Seeing the Lord, they all praised him with joy, bowing down with folded hands. They said: "We bow to and worship Ganesha, from whom the infinite living beings emerge, who is beyond qualities yet the source of all qualities, and from whom the threefold world appears. We worship Ganesha, from whom the creator Brahma, the gods, demons, and humans emerged. We worship Ganesha, from whom the elements, the oceans, the moon, the sky, the wind, and all moving and non-moving beings originate. We worship Ganesha, who is the source of intellect, the destroyer of ignorance, the granter of liberation, the remover of obstacles, and the fulfiller of all works. We worship Ganesha, who grants sons, wealth, devotion, and knowledge, and who is the cause of both sorrow and desire. We worship Ganesha, who is the Supreme Brahman, the embodiment of consciousness and bliss, whom the Vedas describe as 'not this, not this'."

Chapter 92 : The Greatness of Gajanana's Twelve Names Beginning with Sumukha

The King said: Having thus bowed and praised Gajanana, all of them spoke again to the Lord of the Ganas, saying, "Today we are the most fortunate. Our penance, charity, knowledge, and sacrifices, as well as our ancestors, are blessed. Today our eyes are blessed, for they have beheld Gajanana."

Pleased by these nectar-like words and praises, the elephant-faced Lord, out of his profound gravity, spoke to those who were listening: "Such a form of mine, which is beyond attributes (Nirguna), as is being directly witnessed by all of you, has not been seen even by Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva, and others. Being extremely pleased with this praise, I have come here to grant boons. Whatever you desire, ask for it all."

O Sage, having been told this by the formidable Lord, they asked Gajanana for whatever they desired. Because the number of those boons was countless, it is not within the power of even the four-faced Brahma to describe them entirely; therefore, I have stated them briefly.

He granted those various boons that everyone asked of Gajanana. He further said to all, "This hymn is dear to me. Whoever recites it at the three junctures of the day (morning, noon, and evening) shall become learned and blessed with children. He shall attain longevity, health, prosperity, fame, growth, and victory. He shall obtain all desired objects and, in the end, the supreme state. The Lord of the Ganas further said: A person who recites this hymn at the three junctures of the day will see all his tasks accomplished."

"Whoever recites these eight auspicious verses for eight days, eight times on the fourth day (Chaturthi), shall attain the eight Siddhis. Whoever recites it for a month, ten times every day, shall be released from bondage; there is no doubt that he will be freed from royal imprisonment. One desiring knowledge shall obtain knowledge, and one desiring a son shall obtain a son. By reciting it twenty-one times, one obtains everything desired."

Having said this to those who were devoted to Gajanana, the Lord, the foundation of all, the auspicious-faced Gajanana, vanished before the eyes of all. Then they created an auspicious-faced image of Ganesha. Having consecrated it in a grand temple made of jewels, they gave it the well-known name 'Sumukha'. Having worshipped and bowed, the gods went to their respective abodes, and all the sages and people became engaged in their respective duties.

Some explicitly named the image 'Ekadanta'. The Gandharvas and Kinnaras installed another excellent image in a magnificent golden temple, and after worshipping it in many ways, they established for it the excellent name 'Kapila'. The Guhyakas, Charanas, and Siddhas created another image; having consecrated it in a grand hall, they bowed and worshipped it. They gave it the clear name 'Gajakarna'. By its influence, they all ascended to heaven in celestial vehicles.

An image was installed by all humans with the name 'Lambodara'. Another excellent image was installed by all the wild animals, and having worshipped it with the name 'Vikata', they went to the forest. The mountains and trees, having installed and worshipped another image, remained there after naming it 'Vighnanashana' (Destroyer of Obstacles). By his grace, those mountains and trees also became famous.

An image of gold and jewels was installed by all the birds, and they worshipped and bowed to it with the name 'Ganadhipa'. One image was installed by all the serpents, and they clearly called it 'Dhumraketu'. One auspicious image was installed by all the bodies of water, and it was worshipped with great festivities by the name 'Ganadhyaksha'.

A supreme image was installed by the multitude of worms, insects, plants, and herbs, and it became famous as 'Bhalachandra'. Another great image was installed by other sentient beings in the middle of a jewel temple, and the great image of Vainayaki was worshipped with devotion.

Known by the name 'Gajanana', it grants all desires to all. They became famous in the three worlds according to their respective species. They were happy and efficient in their own tasks by the grace of the Lord. I have the power to narrate each name individually. Having taken the essence of the essence, a thousand names were created, and from those, twelve were declared as the most essential.

Just as fourteen jewels were obtained from the churning of the ocean, O Brahma, I have thus described his glory in brief. Even Shesha, I, or Hari cannot describe it in detail. What is the count for creatures from Indra down to a mosquito, or for the Yakshas and Rakshasas? Therefore, O son of Satyavati (Vyasa), Gajanana should be worshipped in all tasks. Whoever does not worship the God of Gods, the destroyer of obstacles, that evil-minded person should be abandoned and kept at a distance like a Chandala.

The Sage said: "Tell me the twelve names in order. By hearing and reciting them, may everything become free of obstacles."

Brahma said: "Sumukha, Ekadanta, Kapila, Gajakarnaka, Lambodara, Vikata, Vighnanasha, Ganadhipa, Dhumraketu, Ganadhyaksha, Bhalachandra, and Gajanana. Whoever recites or hears these twelve names at the beginning of education, marriage, entering or leaving a house, in battle, or in distress, no obstacle arises for him."

"One should meditate on the God who wears white garments, who is the color of the moon, who has four arms, and a pleasant face, for the pacification of all obstacles. The merit of these names does not reach even a hundredth part of the merit of giving away a crore of maidens, performing a crore of sacrifices, vows, penances, visiting all holy places, donating thousands of loads of gold, or performing various difficult austerities. A person who, having risen in the morning, becomes pure and focused, and recites these with devotion, obstacles do not approach him. All his tasks are accomplished, and in the end, he attains liberation. By his sight, the people and gods become purified. Therefore, O Sage, the Saura, Shakta, Shaiva, and Vaishnava sects all perform their tasks after uttering these twelve names. Even the Ganesha-devotees do so; what is the wonder in this? Without the utterance of these twelve names, tasks do not succeed. Therefore, O Brahma, one should utter at least one. Even the wicked and atheists, whose tasks are accomplished, utter the seed-syllable even out of ignorance. Thus, O Sage, all this glory has been described to you in brief. The fruit of worship has been described according to my intellect. As much as was told by Vishnu, that much I have described to you. Even he did not reach the end of this Ganesha worship. The glory of Ganesha's name was not fully known even by him."

Bhrigu said: "O King, I have thus narrated to you the wonderful glory which was described by the pleased Brahma to Vyasa. I have described this section of worship to you, O King. If you have faith in listening, then I shall describe another, the life of Gananatha, which destroys the sins of Somakanta."

Suta said: "Thus, O great sages headed by Shaunaka, I have told you the worship of Ganesha, which is accompanied by various stories. That which was spoken by Brahma to the sage Vyasa, and narrated by Bhrigu to Somakanta, is the destroyer of sins. Whoever hears this excellent Purana of Ganesha, having left behind all calamities and enjoyed various pleasures, accompanied by sons and grandsons, and endowed with knowledge and wisdom, attains supreme liberation by the grace of Ganesha. There is no return for him even after hundreds of crores of kalpas. Whoever narrates this with great devotion shall attain the fruit as described, just as Somakanta attained it by listening with supreme reverence."

Thus ends the ninety-second chapter of the Upasana Khanda in the Ganesha Purana, titled "The Description of the Names of Gajanana."

Dedicated to the blessed Gajanana. Salutations to Sri Sham.

Thus ends the Purva Khanda (Upasana Khanda) of the Srimad Ganesha Maha Purana.
